Basundari Roṇḍon

Authors of digital edition: Muhamad Alnoza & Arlo Griffiths

Filename: DiplEdBasundariRondonOr3583

Language: Old Javanese

Repository: Nusantara Philology (tfd-nusantara-philology)

Version: part commented since without access_token with github actions api calls are limited – still working on it

Witness

  • London, British Library, Lontar, Or. 3583
    • Content:
      • Basundari Roṇḍon
    • Colophon:
      • diplomatic: //0// Itiḥ basundari roṇḍon ri sampun təlas cinitra ri jəṅira saṅ hyaṅ giri damaluṅ ri pacimmā para batuṙ pasantren, ṅ haṅiṁ pasaḥ tan pasaḥ raganipūn, gəṅ iṅ drayā hamor iṅ aṅga aprasadu nista yan iṅ aksa deniṅ antuk asisinahu, samahāhado haparək hanudhat sastra, hajəjəkaṇ ḍupak sastra hadhədhək anmu gatəl, deniṅ aksarā śri gitiṁṅən kadi cinakaṙ riṁ rākāṭā. samantahā tan epatut iṁ tātavraga sastranipun, tan apatut sasānākaliḥ huṁkaranipun, samantahā tan apatut guha paruṅguni śastra, deniṁ cedaksarā punikā sastra kaliḥdaśa, samantahā kiraṅ lviḥ divəg tinata denirā saṁ pratameṁ sastra, mivaḥ kaṅ amahostanapi kaṅərəṅə, hatava hikaṅ analaṙ hamalāmpaḥ iṅ apura pukulun duk ahuvusan iṅ anurat, vu, ma, śu, ka, vu, ka, u, //0// I śakala| 7| 7| 5| 1| //0// Om̐ śri śri śri
    • Physical Description: Palm-leaf manuscript. 80 leaves in Javanese script of the Merapi-Merbabu type. The top and bottom lines of the leaves are darkened and often illegible. The leaves have no original numbering (or it is illegible) and seem to be out of order. The most frequently encountered feature in this manuscript is that nasal sound marks, such as ṁ and n, at the end of words are often ignored by the scribe. In addition, vowels or consonants at the end of a word are sometimes linked to the following word. Meanwhile, diphthong sounds such as "ai" replacing "e" and "au" for "o" tend to be commonly found. Uniquely, the number 8 in this ms. is sometimes written like "pha"—possibly due to the phonetic similarity between the aksara "pha" and "pa", whereas "pa" is more naturally used when writing the number 8.
    • Hand Description: A1:H1 main hand of the manuscript, black ink, Javanese script
    • History: Purchased from B. Farrer, 1888. The manuscript was copied in the hermitage of "Pasantren", located on the western slope of Mount Damalung (nowaday Merbabu), in the Śaka 1557, i.e., 1635 AD

    . <physDesc>
    . <objectDesc>
    70 <!-- Here you can give a physical description of the manuscript in prose-->
    . <p>Palm-leaf manuscript. 80 leaves in Javanese script of the Merapi-Merbabu type. The top and bottom lines of the leaves are darkened and often illegible. The leaves have no original numbering (or it is illegible) and seem to be out of order. The most frequently encountered feature in this manuscript is that nasal sound marks, such as ṁ and n, at the end of words are often ignored by the scribe. In addition, vowels or consonants at the end of a word are sometimes linked to the following word. Meanwhile, diphthong sounds such as "ai" replacing "e" and "au" for "o" tend to be commonly found. Uniquely, the number 8 in this ms. is sometimes written like "pha"—possibly due to the phonetic similarity between the aksara "pha" and "pa", whereas "pa" is more naturally used when writing the number 8.</p>
    . </objectDesc>
    . <handDesc> <!-- Add an attribute @hands if you have more than one hand -->
